Hello, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I, um, I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time today.

And I don't even really enjoy them, to be honest. \n\nIt's silly, I know. I just... I hope it's okay that I came in to talk about it. Is that the sort of thing I should be here for?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I haven't really been keeping strict track on a scale, I hope that's okay. But like I was just saying about my eating being all turned around for months... with just picking at toast some days and then getting all those sweets on others... I think my weight has been going up and down a little bit because of it. My clothes just fit a bit differently depending on the week. \n\nI'm sorry I don't have an exact number for you. I hope I'm giving you the right information... does that make sense, with how my eating has been?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of your eating when you're buying and consuming all those sweets?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I wouldn't say I'm completely out of control. I mean, I know I'm putting them in the cart. It's just... I guess I just get a little carried away sometimes? \n\nIt's really not as bad as it probably sounds, I promise. I just, you know, I'm at the store and I see them, and... well, I just buy them. But I can always stop myself if I really need to. I hope that doesn't make me sound terrible. I know I should have better willpower at my age. Does that answer your question okay? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after eating those sweets?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh... um, well, no, not exactly. I mean, I know I probably shouldn't be eating them, but... I don't really sit there and beat myself up over it. It's more just... I feel a little silly about the whole thing, I guess? \n\nI hope that doesn't make it sound like I don't care about my health. I really do care, I promise. I just don't get that terrible guilty feeling afterwards. Is it a bad thing that I don't feel guilty? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of sweets in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no... um, I don't think I do that. I mean, I might have a few things over the course of the whole day, but I definitely don't sit down and eat a huge amount all at once. \n\nI just sort of... space it out, you know? Just a little nibble here and there. I wouldn't want you to think I'm just gorging myself or anything like that. I really try to be good about not overdoing it in one sitting. Oh, um... yes, I think so. Like I was mentioning just a little bit ago, my eating has just been all turned around for months now. Oh, um... yes, I think you're asking about the same thing again? I'm so sorry, I really hope I'm not frustrating you by not being clear enough. I'm trying to be helpful, I promise.

But yes, when I'm just having the toast, it's really because I'm just not hungry at all. Like I mentioned, my eating has been all turned around for months now... I came in because of it, really. Some days I just pick at the toast and coffee because I have absolutely no appetite, and then other days, like I said, I'm in the bakery aisle buying those sweets I don't even really enjoy. I try to be good and eat normally, I really do, it's just... it's been so unpredictable. Does that answer your question better? I hope I'm giving you the right information.

You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... I'm so sorry, I feel like I'm just repeating myself again, and I really don't want to be a bother. You're being so kind to ask. 

I guess... well, like I was saying before, my eating has just been all turned around for months now. I don't know if it's my emotions, exactly, but it's just so strange. Some days I just pick at a little toast and have my coffee, and I think I'm doing okay, you know? But then... oh, it's a bit embarrassing to admit, but other days I find myself just standing in the bakery aisle, buying all these sweets that I don't even really enjoy that much. I know I shouldn't, and I'm really trying to be better about it. I hope you don't think too poorly of me for that... I'm really trying to keep it under control. Does that help answer your question? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.

I just want to be sure I'm giving you a clear picture of how it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually buy or eat those sweets when you're feeling stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... stressed? No, I don't really think so. I mean, I try to keep a pretty even keel, you know? I definitely don't think I'm reaching for them just because I'm having a bad day or feeling upset. \n\nIt's more just... well, they just taste nice, I suppose. I wouldn't want you to think I'm, you know, eating my feelings or anything like that. I really do try to handle my stress much better than that. Does that make sense? I hope that's helpful... I just haven't really noticed a connection like that, but I can certainly try to pay more attention to it if you want me to!\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mood has any influence on your decision to buy or eat sweets?\n\nPatient: My mood? Oh, um... no, I really don't believe so. I mean, I try very hard not to let my emotions control things like that, you know? It's really just... well, just having a little treat because it tastes nice, not because I'm feeling down or anything. \n\nI know a lot of people might eat when they're sad, but I really try to be good about that. I hope that's alright? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. Is it okay that I don't really see a connection there?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating out of habit or because you're actually hungry when you have those days where you just pick at a piece of toast?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, it's... it's a little bit of both, I suppose. I mean, some days I just don't feel like eating much, so I'll just have a little toast and coffee. I hope that's not too terrible? I just try to listen to my body, you know?\n\nBut... well, honestly, I think I came in because my eating has just gotten all turned around for months now. Like I said, some days it's just picking at toast, but then... well, other days I find myself standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets that I don't even really enjoy. I know that sounds silly, and I really don't want you to think I'm completely out of control. It's just... confusing, I guess. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it okay... I just want to be a good patient and give you the full picture, even if it's a little embarrassing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would change if you were in a different emotional state?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... a different emotional state? I'm... I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I mean, I really try to keep things steady, like I said. I wouldn't want to think that my emotions are dictating everything I do. \n\nI suppose... well, maybe if I felt a bit more energetic, I might cook a proper meal instead of just having toast? But I don't really know if the sweets part would change. I just... I haven't really thought about it like that. I hope that's an okay answer? I'm sorry I don't have a clearer picture for you on that one, I just try to take it one day at a time, you know? Is that what you meant?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself not really wanting to eat because you're just not interested in food?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yes, I think so. I really do try to eat properly, I promise, it's just hard when the desire for it just isn't there. Does that make sense?
